- `2026-10-13T20:32:17` **chief** @20261012-p1-honest: CHIEF RULING R3 (end-of-run exit fee): the exit fee for closing a survivor at run end is a valuation cost, NOT a liquidation trigger. The liquidation hit set is decided by the scan WITHOUT survivor end-of-run exit-fee cash. That fee is booked after the last scanned instant and counts in final equity, total_fees and trade net. If it takes equity below 0, report that honestly: no floor, no extra liquidation, no dropped mark. Applies to screening (T3) and validation (T4). (job p1-honest)  <sub>18de2a6a51415c30-4737</sub>
- `2026-10-13T20:32:58` **chief** @20261012-p1-honest: CHIEF RULING R2b (revises R2's last clause, mode ON only): the scan includes EVERY raw position (closed + all open), with funding through its hit ts or end_ns, because it is real exposure and matters for a cross wallet. If an open SURVIVOR cannot be valued (missing LAST) while liquidation mode is on, FAIL CLOSED: raise a clear error naming the instrument and ts. Never silently exclude it from trades while its funding sits in the ledger. Mode off is unchanged (byte-identical, keeps the old warning+exclude). Applies to T3 and T4. (job p1-honest)  <sub>18de2a73b4d44560-5060</sub>
- `2026-10-13T20:52:40` **chief** @20261012-p1-honest: CHIEF RULING R4 (one scan, one horizon; replaces 2-pass designs). (a) Compute each open position's valuation horizon BEFORE the scan: the ts at which mode-off would mark it (its instrument's last LAST bar <= end) and the funding mode-off would charge it. (b) In mode ON that horizon must lie in the run's final execution bar. If an open position's LAST coverage ends earlier (data gap), FAIL CLOSED with the R2b error naming the instrument, LAST end ts and run end. This extends R2b. (c) Run exactly ONE scan: open positions funded through min(hit, horizon), closed ones as today. Its hit set is final. Survivors then get their end-of-run exit fee (R3) appended after the scan, with no rescan. (d) Consequence: zero-hit ON == OFF in every reported field, and sum(trade net) == ledger equity delta in every case. Applies to T3 (screening) and T4 (validation). (job p1-honest)  <sub>18de2b86f32e1278-0488</sub>
- `2026-10-13T20:52:40` **chief** @20261012-p1-honest: CHIEF RULING (T4 q1): zero-hit ON must equal OFF in every field. The aggregate ValidationResult.gross_pnl is never set on main (always 0.0, a pre-existing reporting bug). Fix it for ALL modes in T4: cost-free gross = net + fees + slippage + funding. Not one of the five exactness metrics. State it in the commit as a reporting fix. (job p1-honest)  <sub>18de2b86f83f0600-0490</sub>
- `2026-10-13T21:04:35` **chief** @20261012-p1-honest: CHIEF RULING R5 (post-horizon exposure). Price exposure runs to end_ns: MARK is scanned to the run end, so a hit after the horizon inside the final bar is real and counts. Funding for every OPEN position (hit or survivor) is cut at min(hit ts, valuation horizon), the mode-off convention. The scan's funding input is the single source of truth: the trade-level metrics for a hit REUSE the scan's funding (truncated at the hit), never re-accrue to the hit ts. Funding through the hit would make the funding input depend on the hit, a circular rescan, so it is rejected. The settlement inside the final bar after the horizon is not charged, matching mode-off. Closed positions are unchanged. Applies to T3 and T4; the brief's VERIFY(d) wording is superseded. (job p1-honest)  <sub>18de2c2d75d8d860-8439</sub>

The run window is [start_ns, end_ns) for EVERYTHING the liquidation layer sees: MARK bars, funding settlements and cash events. A funding settlement at ts >= end_ns is never charged; 'funded to end_ns' in ruling A means settlements < end_ns. No hit can have ts >= end_ns. Enforce this once, in the shared apply.py (drop/raise on funding or cash at ts >= end_ns before the scan) and in screening's gap-position funding cut. - `2026-10-09T19:39:13` **sd** @20261009-mimo-swarm: pandas 3.0.3 rolling(window).sum() is a Kahan-compensated running add/remove plus an all-same-value shortcut (GH#42064: prev_value*nobs) — NO numpy reduction matches it bitwise (window sum, running add/sub, Neumaier, cumsum diff all fail). A Python replica CAN match bitwise but is slower than pandas' C loop, so in exact ports keep just that op on pandas and vectorise the rest; Python-float list recurrences are bitwise-equal to numpy-scalar loops (no FMA fusion across scalar ops) and ~4x faster.  <sub>18dced328da4a0b8-1304</sub>

- `2026-10-09T08:56:23` **chief** @20261009-mimo-swarm: NautilusTrader swallows exceptions inside on_bar → a data error mid-run silently becomes 0 trades. - `2026-10-09T08:57:21` **chief** @20261009-mimo-swarm: Never edit a running shell script in place (bash reads it lazily): cmd-task.sh edited mid-swarm crashed the wrappers' post-processing (line 58 syntax error); agent work survived in worktrees. - `2026-10-17T11:56:14` **cc-gr-hermetic-1** @20261017-grind: Mutant-restore protocol: 'replace the MUTATED text back with the original' corrupts the file when the mutant's new text is a SUBSTRING of the old (e.g. dropping two lines: new='host = sockaddr[0]'), because original.replace(new, old) re-inserts the block around the already-present fragment.
